Course structure AC dated 13.06.2025 pg 215

# Syllabus common for B Tech/ MBA Tech (all programs), can use common question paper.

Essential Electronics Practices - This course empowers students to gather practical knowledge and skills using Internal Continues Assessment (ICA) throughout the semester.

English Communication - This course focuses on introducing the students to the basics of English language and formal communication, which requires to be assessed continuously throughout the semester.

Environmental Studies- The course is for the general awareness about the environment.

Dr. N.T. Rao, Dean, MPSTME J2— elalrerg

From |: | Dr. Manoj Sankhe, HOD, EXTC Engineering, MPSTME

Sub : | Minor Amendments in B. Tech. EXTC Semester VII(Teaching Scheme

Number of practicals and tutorials per week for the subjects ‘Embedded Systems’ and
‘Network Design and Planning’ were amended and the changes were duly approved
in the BOS meeting dated 234 May, 2017. These changes were further approved in the
Academic Council conducted on 23"4 June, 2017. However, in the revised approved
teaching scheme the practical and tutorial components were erroneously typed as
0 and 2, respectively. I request you to kindly approve the revised teaching scheme as
Practical - 2 and Tutorial - 0 for both the above mentioned subjects.

This is applicable for B. Tech. EXTC in 2018-19.

Attached is the old teaching scheme and revised teaching scheme.
Regards,\. _

Dr. Manoj S. Sankhe \L

B.Tech. (EXTC Engineering ) Trimester IV
Sub : Electrical Network Analysis & Code : ENAS
Synthesis
Periods per week Lecture | 4 Hrs.
Practical | -
Tutorial | 2 Hrs.
Hours | Marks
Evaluation Scheme | Theory Examination 3 50 (100 scaled down to 50)
Test 20
Term Work /Pr./Oral 30
Objective:

To provide knowledge of basic fundamentals of Electrical & Electronics network analysis
and synthesis.
Prerequisite : Electronics I & II |

Detailed Syllabus

| Unit | Topics Duration (Hr)
1 Mesh & Node Analysis
Mesh & Node Analysis of circuits with independent & 2

dependent sources.
2 | Network Theorems (Revision)
Linearity, Superposition, Current & Voltage Source
Transformation, Thevenin’s & Norton’s Theorem (with 3
independent & dependent sources), Maximum power transfer
theorem. Miller’s theorem.
3 | Circuit Analysis
Introduction to Graph Theory. Tree, link currents, branch 5
voltages, cut set & tie set. Mesh & Node Analysis, Gauss
Elimination Technique, Duality.
4 Time & Frequency Response of Circuits
First & second Order Differential equations, initial conditions.
Evaluation & analysis of Transient and Steady state responses 10
~ using Classical Technique as well as by Laplace Transform
(for simple circuits only). Transfer function, Concept of poles
and zeros. Frequency response of a system (concepts only).
5 Two — port Networks :
Epncert of two- port network. Driving point & Transfer
unctions, Open Circuit impedance*(Z) parameters, Short
Circuit admittance (Y) parameters, Transmission (ABCD)
parameters. Inverse Transmission (A’B’C’D’) parameters.
Hybrid (h) parameters. Inter Relationships of different
parameters. Interconnections of two — port networks. T & Pi
representation. Terminated two - port networks.

10

2|
--- page 5 ---
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

6 | Fundamentals of Network Synthesis

Positive real functions, Driving Point functions, Brono’s

Positive real functions, Properties of Positive real functions.

Testing Positive real functions, Testing driving point 10
functions, Maximum modulus theorem, Properties of Hurwitz
polynomials, Residue computations, Even & odd functions,

Sturm’s theorem. Driving Point Synthesis with L-C, R-C, R-L

and R-L-C networks.

TOTAL 40

BOOKS
Text Books:

1. M. E. Van Valkenburg, Introduction to Modern Network Synthesis, Wiley Eastern Ltd.
Publication year, 2006 3" edition.

2. William. H. Hayt, Jack E. Kemmerly & Steven M. Durbin, Engineering Circuit Analysis,
McGraw Hill International, sixth edition, 2002.

3. Raymond A. DeCarlo & Pen-Min Lin, Linear Circuit Analysis, Oxford University Press,
second edition, 2001.

Reference Books :

1. A. Sudhakar & S. P. Shyammohan, Circuits and Networks, Tata McGraw Hill, thirteenth
reprint, 2000.

2. Artice M. Davis, Linear Circuit Analysis, Thomson Asia Pte. Ltd., Singapore, first edition,
2001

3. M. E. Van Valkenburg, Network Analysis, Prentice Hall of India, third edition.

Publication year, 2006.

Test & Term Work:
Minimum two class tests. The termwork shall consist of class work in tutorials covering the
whole of syllabus, duly recorded and graded.

B.Tech. (EXTC Engineering ) Trimester - IV

LOGIC CIRCUIT DESIGN Code : LCD

Lecture | 5 Hrs.

Practical | 2 Hrs.

Periods per week

Tutorial | -
Hours Marks
Evaluation Scheme Theory Examination 3 50 (100 scaled down to 50)
Test 20
Term Work / Pr./ Oral 30

Objective:
To provide knowledge of building blocks and their working which is the foundation for digital
system design and applications in computer hardware.

Prerequisite : Electronics I & II

Detailed Syllabus

Unit | Topics Duration (Hrs)

Introduction: 02
1 Number Systems, binary, octal, hexadecimal and others. Conversion from
one system to another, binary, BCD and hexadecimal.

Binary Codes:

2, | Weighted, reflective, sequential, gray, error detecting codes, even parity, 02
Hamming codes, alphanumeric, Morse, teletypewriter ASCII, EBCDIC
codes, converting binary to gray and gray to binary and XS3.

Boolean Algebra Logic Gates:
3, | AND, OR, NOT, XOR, XNOR, operation NAND, NOR use of
| universal gates for performing different operations. Laws Boolean 05
algebra, DeMorgan’s theorems. Relating truth table to a Boolean
expression. Multi level circuits.

Combinational Circuits:
K-maps and their use in specifying Boolean expressions, minterms,
maxterm SOP and POS implementation. Implementing a logic function
4 | using universal gates. Design of Code converters-Binary to Gray, BCD 08
to 7 segments, priority encoder, binary adder, subtractor, BCD Adder,
bi ary comparator, arithmetic logic unit. Variable entered maps for five
aid six variables functions Quine Mc Clusky tabular techniques.

Pleo Logic Circuits Design:

B.Tech. (All Branches) Trimester V
Sub : Seminar —-| Code : S-I
Periods per week Guided Library Work | 4 Hrs.

. Marks
Evaluation Scheme Report (T/W) 50

Objective:
To develop ability to collect information and prepare a report for presentation on a
technical topic.

Prerequisite:
Basic Discipline, subjects upto Trimester IV.

Assignment of topic for the Seminar will be given to each of the students in the class by
one of the teachers teaching the Trimester V subjects. The students will prepare the
detailed report on the Seminar topic by literature survey / self study under the guidance of

the concerned teacher and submit the report for evaluation at the end of the Trimester V.

The report will cover the following :

1. Relevance and importance of the topic
2. Basic Principles / Concepts / Definitions

3. Details of development — Methods / Techniques / Data / Charts / Diagrams
4. Applications — Advantages and Limitations.
5. Discussion of related issues.
6. Future scope

7. References.

Objective: This course introduces the concepts of 8 bit 8051 & 16 bit 80196
microcontrollers and embedded system programming. Also it provides the
understanding of RTOS & embedded system.

Prerequisite: | Elements of Microprocessors
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 Hardware of 8051 Microcontrollers: 4

Comparison of microprocessor and microcontroller,
architecture and pin functions of 8051 chip controiler, CPU
timing and machine cycles, internal memory organization,
program counter and stack, input/output ports, counters and
timers, serial data input and output interrupts.

2 | 8051 Assemble language programming: 6
Instruction set, addressing modes, immediate, registers, direct
and indirect data movement and exchange instructions, push
and pop op-codes, arithmetic and logic instructions, bit level
operations, jump and call instructions, input/ output port
programming, programming timers, asynchronous serial data
communications, timer and hardware interrupt service
routines.

3 | Microcontroller Design and Interfacing: External memory 10
and memory address decoding, memory-mapped I/O, time
4 delay subroutines, look-up table implementation, interfacing
q matrix keyboard and 16x2 characters LCD through scanning
and interrupt driven programs, interfacing analog to digital and
digital to analog converters using handshake signals and
waveform generation interfacing with 8255 I/O , Stepper

Objective: To provide the knowledge of different types of Transforms ( DFT, IDFT, FFT &
DCT) in time & frequency domain and designing of Digital Filters.

Prerequisite : Signals and system

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

1 | Properties of LTI systems: 5
Definition, convolution and solution of Linear difference equations
with constant coefficients. Causality & stability.
Revision of Signals & Systems (incl. Laplace, Fourier & Z-
Transforms)
Frequency response of LTI systems,

2 | Discrete Fourier transform: 7
- DFT and its properties
- linear filtering based on continuous time FT, frequency
analysis of signals using FT
- phase distortion and delay, all pass systems, minimum,
maximum mixed phase systems
- linear phase filters, causal generalized linear phase
system (pole zero plots)
- symmetric, anti-symmetric filters & review of
- lowpass, high pass, band pass filters, frequency
} transformations
yy - digital resonator, comb filters, notch filters, all pass filters
q - digital sinusoidal oscillators

3 | Discrete cosine transform (DCT): 5
-Definition of DCT, DCT-1 and DCT-2
-relationship between FT ,DCT-1 and DCT-2
- energy compaction property of DCT-2, applications of DCT

4 | Computation of DFT & FFT:
- Fast Fourier transform radix 2, radix 4, application
algorithm, efficient computation of DFT of 2N point
sequences, linear filtering, correlation
- Decimation in Time FFT, Decimation-in- Frequency

Inverse FFT, split radix FFT, degradation from finite word length,
round off noise in FFT and IFFT

5 | Design of filters: 8

- Design of FIR filters - windowing method frequency
sampling method, optimum equi-ripple linear phase FIR,
FIR differentiator, optimum approximation of FIR filters

- Hilbert transformers, relationship between no.of coeffients
and filters, characteristics of raised cosine transition filter

- Design of IIR filters: Mapping of differentials, Impulse
invariance, Bilinear transformation, Matched Z-transforms

- Butterworth, Chebyshev filter |, Chebyshev filter Il, and
Elliptic filters

- Frequency transformation low pass to high pass, band
pass, band reject filters.

-__ Design of digital filter based on least square method

6 | Structures for discrete time systems: 8
- FlR structures (direct form, cascade form, frequency
sampling and lattice); structures for linear phase filters.
- Structures for IIR systems, direct form-l, Direct form-ll,
Canonical, Lattice and Lattice ladder structures.
- Basic structure of phase shifters, All-pass filters;
- analysis of cascaded and parallel IIR structures and FIR
structures.

7 | Amplitude quantization : effect of coefficient quantization in IIR 2
and FIR systems, effect of round off noise in digital filters,
quantization errors, limit-cycle oscillations.

8 | Introduction to Programmable Digital Processor 6
DSP processor v/s General purpose processor, multiplier, MAC
unit, Bus Architecture and memory, Pipelining, Multiport memory,
VLIW Architecture, data addressing capabilities, on-chip
Peripherals, P-DSPs with RISC and CISC

Objective: To understand the basic concepts of computer networks. To get an insight into the
layered architecture and see how each layer provides service to the layer above it.

Prerequisite:- Basics principles of
communication.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 Communication networks and services 05

Network functions and network topology, basics of message switching,
packet switching, circuit switching and cell switching. Reference Model
(ISO-OSI, TCP/IP-Overview

2 | Application and layered architectures:

Layering architecture, the OSI reference model, unified view of layers, 05
protocols and services, overview of TCP/IP architecture, TCP/IP
protocol

3 The Physical Layer: 06

Theoretical basis for data communication, transmission media-Magnetic
Media, Twisted Pair, Baseband Coaxial Cable, Broadband Coaxial
Cable, Fibre Cable, Structured Cabling, Cable Mounting, Cable Testing,
Wireless transmission, the telephone system, narrowband ISDN,
broadband ISDN and ATM.

4 | Peer to peer protocols and data link layer: 10

Peer to peer protocols and service models, service models, end to end
requirements and adaptation functions, end to end versus hop by hop.
ARQ protocols, stop and wait ARQ, goback-N ARO, selective repeat

‘ Wy RO, transmission efficiency of ARQ protocols, sliding windows flow
control, timing recovery for synchronous services, reliable stream
service, data link controls, HOLC data link control, point to point
protocol, statistical multiplexing.
The Data Link Layer: Data link layer design issues, error detection and
correction, data link protocols, sliding window protocols, Examples of

Data Link Protocols.

The Medium Access Sublayer: 07
The channel allocation problem, multiple access protocols, IEEE

standard 802 for LANS and MANS, high-speed LANs, satellite

networks, Network devices-repeaters, hubs, switches and bridges.

Packet switching networks: 07
Network services and internal network operation, packet network

topology, connectionless packet switching, virtual circuit packet

switching, routing in packet networks, routing algorithm classification,

routing tables; hierarchical routing, link state versus distance vector

routing, shortest path algorithms, the Bellman-ford algorithm, Dijkstra's

algorithm, other routing approaches.

TCP/IP: 10
The Internet Protocol (IP), IP packet, IP addressing, subnet mask,
classless interdomain routing (CIDR), address resolution, reverse
address resolution, IP fragmentation and reassembly, ICMP, User Date
gram Protocol (UDP), Transmission Control Protocol (TCP), TCP
Reliable stream service, TCP operation, TCP protocol, Dynamic Host
Configuration Protocol (DHCP), mobile IP IPv6, internet routing
protocols, routing information protocols, open shortest path first
protocol, border gateway protocol, multicast routing, reverse path
broadcasting, internet group management protocol, reverse path
multicasting, distance vector multicast routing protocol.

TOTAL 50

elements near or on infinite perfect conductors, ground effects
and their applications

- Principle of pattern multiplication , linear arrays of n elements
- Broadside and end fire radiation pattern, directivity, beam width
and null directions, array factor
- Planer array, circular array and their application

Objective: The subject concentrates on the principles of satellite communication systems,
link design, multiple access technologies and growth of VSAT and GPS systems. To provide
knowledge of radar communication. Study of different types of radar systems. Application of
radar systems for navigation and remote sensing.

Prerequisite Principles of Communication Engineering,
EWT.

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

General background , frequency allocations tur satellite services,
basic satellite system , system design considerations
.applications

, orbital perturbations, Doppler effect , geostationary orbit ,
antenna look angles, limits of visibility, sun transit outrage ,
inclined orbits, sun-synchronous orbit, launching of
geostationary satellites.

3 WAVE PROPAGATION AND LINK DESIGN 10

attenuation, other impairments, antenna polarization ,
polarization of satellite signals, cross polarization
discrimination, ionospheric depolarization, rain depolarization,
ice depolarization.transmission losses, link power budget
equation, system noise, carrier to noise ratio “or uplink and down
link, combined uplink and downlink carrier to noise ratio, inter
modulation noise.

4 COMMUNICATION SATELLITES and EARTH 05
STATIONS

frequency reuse.Earth station general configuration and
characteristics

Introduction, FXOMA, TDMA, FDMA/TDMA, operation in a
multiple beam environment, multiple access example.

6 NON GEOSTATIONARY ORBIT SATELLITE SYSTEM 03

Scheme
Test 20

Objective: The course provides grounding in digital filter and transforms techniques for
image processing and feature extraction. An overview of common heuristic algorithms for
image processing. The different representations of digital images, the importance of
adequate sampling frequencies and the appearance of artifacts. Also how the important
features in an image may be related to significant abstractions from the raw image.

Prerequisite: Digital Signal Processing

Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 Digital Image Processing Systems: 4

Introduction, structure of human eye, imace formation in the
human eye, Brightness adaptation and discrimination, Image
sensing and acquisition, storage, Processing, Communication,
Display Image Sampling and quantization, Basic relationships
between pixels.

2 | Image Transforms (implementation): 8
Introduction to Fourier transform, DFT and 2-D DFT,
Properties of 2-D DFT, FFT, IFFT, Walsh transform,
Hadamard transform, Discrete cosine transform, Slant
transform, Optimum transform: Karhunen — Loeve (Hotelling)
transform.

3 | Image Enhancement in the Spatial and Frequency Domain: 7
Gray level transformations, Histogram processing, Arithmetic
and logic operations, Spatial filtering: Introduction, Smoothing
and sharpening filters. Frequency domain filters: Smoothing
and Sharpening filters, Homomorphic filtering.

4 | Image Data Compression: 7
Fundamentals, Redundancies: Coding, Interpixel Pysycho-
visual, fidelity criteria, Image compression models, Error free
compression, Lossy compression, Image compression
standards: Binary image and Continuous tone still image
compression standards, video compression standards.

5 | Morphological Image Processing: 7
Introduction, Dilation, Erosion, Opening, closing, Hit —or-Miss
transformation, Morphological algorithm operations on binary

images, Morphological algorithm operations on gray-scale

fier

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

images

6 | Image Segmentation, Representation and Description: 7
Detection of discontinuities, Edge linking and Boundary
detection, Thresholding Region based segmentation, Image
Representation schemes, Boundary descriptors, and Regional
descriptors.
Total 40

Text Books:

1. R.C Gonzalez and Richard Woods, Digital Image Processing, 7" Indian reprint,
Pearson publication, 2001.

2. Anil K. Jain, Digital Image processing, PHI.

3. M. A. Sid-Ahmed, Image Processing- Theory, Algorithms & Architecture, McGraw
Hill.

2. Minimum 7 practical experiments covering all the topics.

1 Basics of Simulation Modeling: 5

System model and Simulation, Physical and Mathematical models.
Analytical solutions and simulation output. Continuous and Discrete
simulation models. .

Discrete event simulation: Components and organization of discrete event
scheduling and time advance approaches.

2 Complex system Simulation:
Steps in simulation study, combined discrete continuous simulation, Monte 5
Carlo Simulation. List processing in Simulation, storing list, linked storage
location, simple C based Simulation language Simlib. Single server queueing
Simulation, Simlib program steps and Simul:.‘ion output

Classification of Simulation software general purpose vs Application
oriented Simulation packages. Modeling approaches, common modeling
elements, entities attributes, resources and queries. Validation of
Simulation model.

capabilities, documentation, output reports and graphics, general purpose
Simulation package: Arena, Object Oriented Simulation: Modsim

4 Random process: 5
Random variables, probability density functions and probability distribution
functions. Random members and their properties, Randomness in message
arrival time, type and length etc Review of sampling theorem
for band limited signal and its representation using MATLAB, generation of
random variables for specified probability density function. Gaussian process
in communication system, generation of a muitivariable Gaussian process
and its Simulation using MATLAB, Low and Band pass process
Simulation.

synthesizer design.

Objective:
To develop ability to collect information and prepare a report for presentation on a
technical topic.

Prerequisite:
Basic Discipline, subjects up to Trimester VI.

Assignment of topic for the Seminar will be given to each of the students in the class by
one of the teachers teaching the Trimester VII subjects. The students will prepare the
detailed report on the Seminar topic by literature survey / self study under the guidance of
the concerned teacher and submit the report for evaluation at the end of the Trimester
VU.

The report will cover the following:

1. Relevance and importance of the topic

2. Basic Principles / Concepts / Definitions

3. Details of development — Methods / Techniques / Data / Charts / Diagrams
4. Applications — Advantages and Limitations.

5. Discussion of related issues,

6. Future scope

7. References.

Objective: This course introduces the concepts of 8 bit 8051 & 16 bit 80196
microcontrollers and embedded system programming. Also it provides the understanding of
RTOS & embedded system.

Prerequisite: ‘| Elements of Microprocessors
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 Hardware of 8051 Microcontrollers: 4

Comparison of microprocessor and microcontroller, architecture and
pin functions of 8051 chip controller, CPU timing and machine
cycles, internal memory organization, program counter and stack,
input/output ports, counters and timers, serial data input and output
interrupts.

2 | 8051 Assemble language programming: 6
Instruction set, addressing modes, immediate, registers, direct and
indirect data movement and exchange instructions, push and pop op-
codes, arithmetic and logic instructions, bit level operations, jump
and call instructions, input/ output port programming, programming
timers, asynchronous serial data communications, timer and
hardware interrupt service routines.

3 | Microcontroller Design and Interfacing: External memory and 10
memory address decoding, memory-mapped I/O, time delay
subroutines, look-up table implementation, interfacing matrix
keyboard and 16x2 characters LCD through scanning and interrupt
driven programs, interfacing analog to digital and digital to analog
converters using handshake signals and waveform generation
interfacing with 8255 I/O _, Stepper motor{Interfacing).

4 | Example of an Advanced Microcontroller ( 80C196): Features of 10
MCS-80C196 family microcontrollers, hardware architecture and
pin functions of 80C196, interrupts, addressing modes, instruction
sets and process status word, peripherals and their applications,
serial port, analog to digital converter, pulse width modulator
output, standard I/O ports, high speed input and output including
timers, power down mode and idle modes of microcontroller
operation program protection features using
clock failure detect and watch dog timer.
--- page 3 ---
aS

5 Embedded software: Examples of embedded system, their 5
characteristics and their typical hardware components, embedded
Software architectures, round —robin, round robin with interrupts,
function queue scheduling and real-time operating system, selection.
Real time operating system: Tasks and task states, tasks and data
shared data and reentrancy, semaphores and shared data use of
semaphores, protecting shared data

6 Features of real-time operating system: Messages, queues, >
mailboxes and pipes, timer functions, events memory management,
internal basic system design using RTOS, design principles,
interrupt routines, task structure and task priority & case study of
Embedded systems

Total 40

Text Book:
1. Microcontroller & Embedded system — Muhammad Ali Mazidi
Reference Books:

1. An Embedded Software Primer- David E. Simon, Pearson Education Asia
Publication

2. Embedded Microcontrollers and Processors Manual — Intel Corporation

3. Programming and customizing the 8051 Microcontroller- Myke, Tata Mc Graw
Hill publication

4. Advanced Microprocessors and Interfacing- Badri Ram, Tata Mc Graw Hill
publication

2. Two class tests
3. Minimum two assignments

Objective: To provide the knowledge of different types of Transforms ( DFT, IDFT, FFT & DCT)
in time & frequency domain and designing of Digital Filters.

Prerequisite : Signals and system

Detailed Syllabus
eq Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
Yee 1 | Properties of LTI systems: 5
Definition, convolution and solution of Linear difference equations with
constant coefficients. Causality & stability.
Revision of Signals & Systems (incl. Laplace, Fourier & Z-Transforms)
Frequency response of LTI systems,
2 | Discrete Fourier transform: if
- DFT and its properties
- linear filtering based on continuous time FT, frequency analysis
of signals using FT
- phase distortion and delay, all pass systems, minimum,
maximum mixed phase systems
- linear phase filters, causal generalized linear phase system (pole
zero plots)
- symmetric, anti-symmetric filters & review of
- low pass, high pass, band pass filters, frequency transformations
- digital resonator, comb filters, notch filters, all pass filters
€ -__ digital sinusoidal oscillators
3 | Discrete cosine transform (DCT): 5

-Definition of DCT, DCT-1 and DCT-2
-relationship between FT ,DCT-i and DCT-2
- energy compaction property of DCT-2, applications of DCT

4 | Computation of DFT & FFT: . 9
- Fast Fourier transform radix 2, radix 4, application of FFT algorithm,
efficient computation of DFT of 2N point real sequences, linear
filtering, correlation
- Decimation in Time FFT, Decimation-in-Frequency FFT, Inverse
FFT, split radix FFT, degradation from finite word length, round off
noise in FFT and IFFT
--- page 5 ---
5 | Design of filters: 8
- Design of FIR filters - windowing method frequency sampling
method, optimum equi-ripple linear phase FIR, FIR
differentiator, optimum approximation of FIR filters
jj - Hilbert transformers, relationship between no.of coeffients and
j filters, characteristics of raised cosine transition filter
- Design of IIR filters: Mapping of differentials, Impulse
invariance, Bilinear transformation, Matched Z-transforms
- Butterworth, Chebyshev filter 1, Chebyshev filter II, and Elliptic
filters
- Frequency transformation low pass to high pass, band pass,
band reject filters.
- Design of digital filter based on least square method

6 | Structures for discrete time systems: 8

- FIR structures (direct form, cascade form, frequency sampling
and lattice); structures for linear phase filters.

- Structures for IR systems, direct form-1, Direct form-II,
Canonical, Lattice and Lattice ladder structures.

- Basic structure of phase shifters, All-pass filters;

~ - analysis of cascaded and parallel IIR structures and FIR

structures.

7 | Amplitude quantization : effect of coefficient quantization in IIR and 2
FIR systems, effect of round off noise in digital filters, quantization
errors, limit-cycle oscillations.

8 | Introduction to Programmable Digital Processor 6
DSP processor v/s General purpose processor, multiplier, MAC unit,
Bus Architecture and memory, Pipelining, Multiport memory, VLIW
Architecture, data addressing capabilities, on-chip Peripherals, P-DSPs
with RISC and CISC

1 | Introduction to Base band transmission :

Objective: To understand the basic concepts of computer networks. To get an insight into the
| layered architecture and see how each layer provides service to the layer above it.

Prerequisite:- Basics principles of
communication.

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

1 | Communication networks and services 05
Network functions and network topology, basics of message switching,
packet switching, circuit switching and cell switching. Reference Model
(ISO-OSI, TCP/IP-Overview

2 | Application and layered architectures:

Layering architecture, the OSI reference model, unified view of layers, 05
protocols and services, overview of TCP/IP architecture, TCP/IP
protocol

3. | The Physical Layer: 06

Theoretical basis for data communication, transmission media-Magnetic
Media, Twisted Pair, Baseband Coaxial Cable, Broadband Coaxial
Cable, Fibre Cable, Structured Cabling, Cable Mounting, Cable Testing,
Wireless transmission, the telephone system, narrowband ISDN,
broadband ISDN and ATM.

4 | Peer to peer protocols and data link layer: 10
Peer to peer protocols and service models, service models, end to end
requirements and adaptation functions, end to end versus hop by hop.

ARQ protocols, stop and wait ARQ, goback-N ARO, selective repeat
ARO, transmission efficiency of ARQ protocols, sliding windows flow
control, timing recovery for synchronous services, reliable stream
service, data link controls, HOLC data link control. point to point
protocol, statistical multiplexing.

The Data Link Layer: Data link layer design issues, error detection and
corseetion, data link protocols, sliding window protocols, Examples of

‘Data Link Protocols.
--- page 3 ---
The Medium Access Sublayer:

The channel allocation problem, multiple access protocols, [EEE
standard 802 for LANS and MANS, high-speed LANs, satellite
networks, Network devices-repeaters, hubs, switches and bridges.

07

Packet switching networks:

Network services and internal network operation, packet network

topology, connectionless packet switching, virtual circuit packet

switching, routing in packet networks, routing algorithm classification.
| routing tables: hierarchical routing. link state versus distance vector

routing, shortest path algorithms, the Bellman-ford algorithm, Dijkstra's

algorithm, other routing approaches.

07

TCP/IP:

The Internet Protocol (IP), [IP packet, IP addressing, subnet mask,

classless interdomain routing (CIDR). address resolution, reverse
| address resolution, IP tragmentation and reassembly, ICMP, User Date
gram Protocol (UDP), Transmission Control Protocol (TCP), TCP
Reliable stream service. TCP operation, TCP protocol, Dynamic Host
Configuration Protocol (DHCP), mobile [P IPvo, internet routing
protocols, routing information protocols, open shortest path first
protocol, border gateway protocol, multicast routing, reverse path
broadcasting, internet group management protocol, reverse path
multicasting, distance vector multicast routing protocol.

10

Objective: Antennas and wave propagation plays an important role in telecommunication. This
course is designed to cover the basics of Antenna and various types of antennas and the theory
of Wave Propagation.

Prerequisite Electromagnetic Wave theory
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 Basic antenna concepts: 06
- Introduction

- Basic antenna parameters, radiation pattern, radiation power density,
radiation intensity. directivity, beam efficiency, gain, aperture concept,
effective height, polarization, input impedance, radiation efficiency

- FRIIS transmission equation

2 | Radiation integrals:
- Basic concepts of Maxwell’s equation 04
- Vector potential, wave equation
- Near field and far field radiation
- Dual equations for electric and magnetic current sources

3 | Linear wire antennas: - 08

- Infinitesimal dipole, its radiation field, radiation resistance,
radiation distance, radiation sphere, near field, far field
directivity

- small dipole, finite length dipole, half wave length dipole, linear
elements near or on infinite perfect conductors, ground effects
and their applications

- Folded dipole, sleeve dipole and their application

4 Arrays: 10
- Array of two isotropic point sources, non isotropic sources
- Principle of pattern multiplication , linear arrays of n elements

Broadside and end fire radiation pattern, directivity, beam width

and null directions, array factor

-°; Planer array, circular array and their application
--- page 5 ---
5 | Different types of antennas: 16
- Loop antenna: Smal! loop comparison of small loop with short
dipole, radiation pattern, its parameters and their applications
- Traveling wave and broad band antennas: V antenna, rhombic
antenna, yagi uda array
- Log periodic Antenna
- Aperture antennas: Rectangular and circular aperture, horn
antennas
- Reflector antennas: Plane reflector. corner reflector, parabolic
reflector and their applications
| - Microstrip antennas: Basic characteristics, feeding methods
rectangular, rectangular patch, circular patch, triangular patch.
quality factor, bandwidth and efficiency and their application
6 | Wave propagation: 06

- Structure of atmosphere, Ground waves, Sky waves.
ionospheric propagation, virtual height, MUF
- Space wave propagation, Forward scatter propagation

models.

aspects, network operations

telephone, CT2, DECT, PHS, PACS

7 CDMA digital cellular standard (18-95): 4
Frequency and channel specifications, forward and reverse
CDMA channel.

Over view, types, radiated power, functional architecture,

Sub : Data Compression and Encryption

Objective:
Prerequisite | Principles of Communication Engineering
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration (Hr)
1 | Data Compression and Encryption: 4

Need for Data Compression, Lossy/ lossless compression,
symmetrical compression and compression ratio, run length
encoding (RLE) for text and image compression, relative encoding
and its applications in facsimile data compression and telemetry,
scalar quantization

2 | Statistical Methods:
Statistical modeling of information source, coding redundancy, 6
variable size codes prefix codes, Shannon-Fano coding, Huffman
coding, adaptive Huffman coding, arithmetic coding a adaptive
arithmetic coding, text
compression using PPM method.

3 | Dictionary Methods: . 6
String compression, sliding window compression, LZ77, LZ78and
LZW algorithms and applications in text compression, Zip and Gzip,
ARC and Redundancy code.

4 | Image compression: 7
Loss less techniques of image compression, gray codes, two
dimensional image transform, discrete cosine transform and its
application in lossy image compression, quantization, zig-zag "
coding sequences, JPEG and JPEG-LS compression standards, pulse
code modulation and differential pulse code modulation methods of
image compression, video compression and MPEG industry
standard.

5 | Audio Compression: 5
Digital Audio, Lossy sound compression, M-Iaw and A-law
compounding, DPCM and ADPCM audio compression, MPEG
audio standard, frequency domain coding, format of compressed
data.
--- page 7 ---
«>

6 | Conventional Encryption: 5

Security of information, security attacks, classical techniques,
Caesar cipher, block ciper principles, data encryption standard, key
generation for DES, block cipher principle, design and modes of
operation, S-box design, triple DES with two three keys,
introduction to international data encryption algorithm, key
distribution.

7 | Number Theory and public encryption: 4

Modular arithmetic, Fermat's and Euler's theorems, Chinese
remainder theorem, discrete logarithm, principles of public key
cryptosystems, RSA algorithm, key management, Diffie-Hellman
key exchange, elliptic curve cryptography.

8 | Message Authentication: 3

Authentication requirements and functions, message authentication
codes (MAC), hash functions and their security, hash and MAC
algorithms, digital signatures and authentication protocols, digital
signature standard and algorithms.

Total 40

Publication

Objective: The subject concentrates on the principles of satellite communication systems,
link design, multiple access technologies and growth of VSAT and GPS systems. To
provide knowledge of radar communication. Study of different types of radar systems.
Application of radar systems for navigation and remote sensing.

Prerequisite Principles of Communication Engineering,
EWT.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 | INTRODUCTION TO SATELLITE COMMUNICATION 02

General background , frequency allocations for satellite services,
basic satellite system , system design considerations ,applications

2 | SATELLITE ORBITS.
Introduction, laws governing satellite motion , orbital parameters , 05
orbital perturbations, Doppler effect , geostationary orbit , antenna
look angles, limits of visibility, sun transit outrage , inclined
orbits, sun-synchronous orbit, launching of geostationary
satellites.

3. | WAVE PROPAGATION AND LINK DESIGN 10
Introduction , atmospheric losses, ionospheric effects, rain
attenuation, other impairments, antenna polarization , polarization
of satellite signals, cross polarization discrimination, ionospheric
depolarization, rain depolarization, ice depolarization.transmission
losses, link power budget equation, system noise, carrier to noise
ratio for uplink and down link, combined uplink and downlink
carrier to noise ratio, inter modulation noise.

4 | COMMUNICATION SATELLITES and EARTH STATIONS 05
Introduction, lifetime and reliability, spacecraft sub systems,
frequency reuse.Earth station general configuration and
characteristics

5 | MULTIPLE ACCESS TECHNIQUE 05
| Introduction, FOMA, TDMA, FDMA/TDMA, operation in a
multiple beam environment, multiple access example.
--- page 3 ---
6 | NON GEOSTATIONARY ORBIT SATELLITE SYSTEM

Introduction, reasons, design considerations, example of systems

DTH,VSAT and GPS

7 Introduction to Radar : Radar set, radar frequcicies and
radar applications.

Range performance, minimum detectabie signai, receiver
noise, transmitter power, pulse repetition frequency, pulse
duration, system losses.

8 | Radar Targets:
Introduction to Radar cross-section.

9 CW, MT! & Pulse Radars:

CW radars, FMCW radar, MFCW radar, MTI radar, Pulse
Doppier radar -

Principles, operation, performance, limitations and
applications.

Radar Clutters:

Surface clutter radar equations, sea clutter, land clutter,
effects of weather on radar angles echoes.

Total |

Text Books
1. Satellite Communication — Timothy Pratt — Wiley Publication
2. Satellite Communication — Dennis Roddy
3. Radar Systems and Radio Aids to Navigation - A. K. Sen & A. B.
Bhattacharya Khanna publication.
4. Itroduction to Radar System - M. L. Skohlik, Mc-Graw Hill publication

Reference Books:
1. Mobile Satellite Communication — Principles and trends — M Richharia —
2. Satellite Communication — Tri. T. Ha
3. Modern Radar system analysis — D K Barton — Artech House 1988
4. Radar: Principles, Technology, Applications - B Adde — Prentice Hall 199

Test & Term Work:
Minimum two class tests. The termwork shall consist of at least eight lal
experiments covering the whole of syllabus, duly recorded and graded.

Objective: The course provides grounding in digital filter and transforms techniques for
image processing and feature extraction. An overview of common heuristic algorithms for
image processing. The different representations of digital images. the importance of
adequate sampling frequencies and the appearance of artifacts. Also how the important
features in an image may be related to significant abstractions from the raw image.

Prerequisite: Digital Signal Processing

Detailed Syliabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 | Digital Image Processing Systems: 4

Introduction, structure of human eye, image formation in the
human eye, Brightness adaptation and discrimination, Image
sensing and acquisition, storage, Processing, Communication,
Display Image Sampling and quantization, Basic relationships
between pixels.

2 | Image Transforms (implementation): 8
Introduction to Fourier transform, DFT and 2-D DFT, Properties
of 2-D DFT, FFT, IFFT, Walsh transform, Hadamard transform,
Discrete cosine transform. Slant transform. Optimum transform:
Karhunen — Loeve (Hotelling) transform.

3 | Image Enhancement in the Spatial and Frequency Domain: 7
Gray level transformations. Histogram processing. Arithmetic and
logic operations, Spatial filtering: Introduction, Smoothing and
sharpening filters. Frequency domain filters: Smoothing and
Sharpening filters. Homomorphic filtering.

4 | Image Data Compression: 7
Fundamentals, Redundancies: Coding, Interpixel Pysycho-visual,
fidelity criteria, Image compression’ models, Error free
compression, Lossy compression, Image compression standards:
Binary image and Continuous tone still image compression
standards. video compression standards. _|

5 | Morphological Image Processing: 7
Introduction, Dilation, Erosion, Opening. closing, Hit —or-Miss
transformation, Morphological algorithm operations on binary
images, Morphological algorithm operations on gray-scale images

6 |Image Segmentation, Representation and Description: 7
Detection of discontinuities, Edge linking and Boundary detection,
Thresholding Region based segmentation, Image Representation
schemes, Boundary descriptors, and Regional descriptors.

40

41. R.C Gonzalez and Richard Woods, Digital Image Processing, 7 Indian reprit

2. Anil K. Jain, Digital Image processing, PHI.

Prerequisite |
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration (Hr)
1 | Telephony Background: 10
Analogue networks, switching systems, transmission systems,
modulation,

multiplexing, signaling, subscriber loop design, calculating resistance
Limit, calculating loss limit, voice digitization, pulse code modulation
(PCM), time division multiplexing, high order PCM multiplex systems,
traffic analysis, traffic characterization, arrival and holding time, loss
systems, lost calls, network blocking probabilities, delay systems,
measurement of traffic congestion, lost calls and grade of service,
availability. Handling of calls, infinite and finite traffic sources,
probability distribution curves, erlange and Poisson traffic formulas.

2 | Digital switching and networks:
Space division switching, time division switching, time space time (TST) 4
switch, space time space (STS) switch, comparison of TST and STS
switches, network synchronization, control and management, timing
inaccuracies, network synchronization, network control.

3 Integrated service digital network (ISDN): 8
ISDN overview, conceptual network, ISDN protocol structure and
standards, ISDN interfaces and functions, transmission structure, user
network interface, ISDN protocol architecture, ISDN connection,
addressing, internetworking, ISDN physical layer: basic rate user network
interface primary rate user network interface, U interface, ISDN data link
layer: LPAD protocol, terminal adaptation, bearer channel data link
control, ISDN network layer: basic call control, control of supplementary
services, signaling system no. 7 (SS7): signaling date link level, signaling
link level, network level, ISDN signaling connection control part, ISDN
user port, broadband ISDN(B - ISDN): (B - ISDNS) standards,
architecture protocol reference model, B-ISDN, physical layer.

4 | Access Technologies: 4
High-speed digital subscriber loops (DSL), ADSL, HDSL, VDSI, wireless
local loop (WLL).
--- page 12 ---
5 | Voice over IP: 8
TCP/IP protocol family, internet protocol (IP) header, IP addressing,
internet control manage message protocol (ICMP), transport protocol,
quality of service on IP, Ethernet frame, LAN queing, resource
reservation
protocol (RSVP), multi protocol label switching, real time protocol
(RTP), session initiation protocol (SIP). H.323 standard media gateway
control protocol, voice over IP gateway case studies, voice coding,
properties of speech, waveform coding, vocoding, hybrid coding,
telephony, signaling call set up, local loop. PBX interfaces.

6 | Voice over other packet networks: 6
Voice over frame relay (VoFR), frame relay frame format, frame length
handling, fragmentation, frame prioritization, frame loss handling, echo
cancellation, silence suppression telephony signaling, VoFR standard,
voice over A TM, A TM cell format, A TM protocol stack, ATM
adaptation layer, IP over ATM, frame relay over ATM.

Total _ 40

Objective:
To develop ability to collect information and prepare a report for presentation on a
technical topic.

Prerequisite:
Basic Discipline, subjects upto Trimester IV. x

Assignment of topic for the Seminar will be given to each of the students in the class by
one of the teachers teaching the Trimester V subjects. The students will prepare the
detailed report on the Seminar topic by literature survey / self study under the guidance of

the concerned teacher and submit the report for evaluation at the end of the Trimester V.

The report will cover the following :

1. Relevance and importance of the topic

2. Basic Principles / Concepts / Definitions

3. Details of development — Methods / Techniques / Data / Charts / Diagrams
4. Applications — Advantages and Limitations.

5. Discussion of related issues,

6. Future scope

7. References. Z \

Objective:
To provide the knowledge of electromagnetic wave theory through the basics of electrostatics
and magnetostatics and plane wave propagation characteristics.

Prerequisite: Basic Electrical Engineering, MathsI& IV |

Detailed syllabus

Unit Topics Duration (Hr)

1 Introduction to Vector Algebra: Vector Field, Rectangular, 3
Cylindrical and Spherical Coordinate systems.

Coulomb’s law and electric field intensity: Coulomb’s law,
2 electric field intensity, calculation of electric field intensity for 5
various charge distributions, streamlines and sketches of field.

Electric flux density and Gauss’s law: Electric flux density ,
Gauss’s law, vector operator and divergence theorem.

Energy and potential: Energy expended in moving a point charge
in an electric field, line integral, line integral, potential and potential
difference, calculations of electric field of both point charge and
system of charges, potential gradient, dipole, energy density.

Conductors, Dielectrics, capacitance: Current and current density
continuity of current, conductor properties, dielectric material and
properties, capacitance, capacitance, calculation of capacitance of
various configurations method of images.

Poisson and Laplace’s equations: Poisson and Laplace’s equation
6 and its application, uniqueness theorem, product solution of 5
Laplace’s equation.

Steady magnetic field: Biot- Savart law, Ampere’s circuital law,
7 curl of H, Stoke’s theorem, magnetic flux and flux density, scalar 6
and vector magnetic potentials.

Time varying fields and Maxwell’s equations: Faraday’s law
concept of displacement currents, Maxwell’s equations in point
form, Maxwell’s equations in integral form, boundary conditions
and significance of Maxwell’s equations.

Uniform Plane waves: Uniform plane waves in time domain in free
space, sinusoidally time varying uniform plane Waves in free space,
9 | wave equation, wave equation and solution for material uniform 6
plane, Waves in dielectrics and conductors, reflection of uniform
plane waves, polarization of waves.

Poynting vector and flow of power: Poynting vector and flow of
10 | power: Poynting theorem, power flow for a plane wave, Poynting 3
loss in a plane conductor.

Total 50

To develop presentation skill and share the knowledge.

Prerequisite:
Seminar - |

Each student will prepare and make Power Point Presentation in the class
on the report already submitted in Seminar |, which will be attended by all
the students and 2 Teacher Examiners. About 30 minutes time will be

allotted for presentation and 15 minutes for question and answers.
